\mathsf{res}^{\mathbf{u}}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{\mu})=\mathsf{res}^{\mathbf{v}}_{\ell}(\boldsymbol{\nu})
, and hence

\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\iota_{\ell,e}(\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{t}))=\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{u})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\nu};\mathbf{v})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\iota_{\ell,e}(\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{t}))

as desired.

∎

Recall the Ariki-Koike algebra
\mathcal{H}_{n}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{F},q,\mathbf{r}}(n)
.

Lemma 3.10
.

Let
w_{0}\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}\in\mathcal{A_{\mathsf{F}}}\cap\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}
, and let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
.

(1)

Then

\{\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})\mid w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell},\,\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}\}=\{(\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}}))^{\rho_{\ell}^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\}.

(2)

If
\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\mu}
lie in the same block
B
of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
and
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
, then
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w^{\prime}})
for all
w,w^{\prime}\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}=\mathbf{r}^{w^{\prime}}=\mathbf{r}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from Lemma
3.7
(2).

For part (2), if
\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\mu}
lie in the same block, then

\displaystyle\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})

\displaystyle=\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w}|}(\mathsf{core}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}))

\displaystyle=\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w}|}(\mathsf{core}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w})

\displaystyle\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w})

by Theorem
2.9
,
so that
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w})
by Lemma
3.1
(2).
Consequently,

\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w^{\prime}})

by Lemma
3.7
(3).
∎

Definition 3.11
.

Given a block of
B
of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
,
we shall denote the common moving vector
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
and all
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}^{\ell}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w}=\mathbf{r}^{\prime}
in Lemma
3.10
(2) as

\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)=(\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}_{1}(B),\dotsc,\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}_{\ell}(B)).

Corollary 3.12
.

Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, and let
\mathbf{r}^{\mathcal{H}}\in\mathcal{A_{\mathsf{F}}}\cap\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}
. Then

\{\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)\mid\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}^{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}\}=\{(\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\mathcal{H}}}(B))^{\rho_{\ell}^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\}.

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
B
and
C
be two blocks of Ariki-Koike algebras with the same
\ell
-charge
\mathbf{r}
.
Let
\hat{w}\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
.
Then
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(C)
if and only if
B=w\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}C
for some
w\in\mathbf{W}_{e}
.

Proof.

If
B=w\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}C
where
w\in\mathbf{W}_{e}
, then for
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
, we have
w\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lies in
C
, and so

\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(C)=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((w\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}(w\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}}))=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)

by Lemma
3.9
(2).

Conversely, suppose that
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(C)
.
By Lemma
3.9
(1), we can find
B_{0}\in\mathbf{W}_{e}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
and
C_{0}\in\mathbf{W}_{e}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}C
such that
\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B_{0}),\mathsf{hub}_{j}(C_{0})\leq 0
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
be lying in
B_{0}
.
Then

\mathsf{hub}_{j}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}})=\mathsf{hub}^{\mathcal{H}}_{j}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B_{0})\leq 0

for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
.
Consequently
\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}})=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\varnothing}))
for some unique
\mathbf{t}^{*}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
by Lemma
2.5
.
By Corollary
3.5
,
\mathbf{t}^{*}
is completely determined by
\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}
and
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B_{0})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)
. Consequently
\mathsf{core}_{e}(B_{0})=\beta^{-1}(\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\hat{w}}))=\beta^{-1}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\varnothing})))
is completely determined by
\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}
and
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)
.

Similar statements hold for
C_{0}
, so that
\mathsf{core}_{e}(B_{0})=\mathsf{core}_{e}(C_{0})
since
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(C)
.
Since

\mathsf{wt}_{e}(B_{0})=|\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B_{0})|=|\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(B)|=|\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(C)|=|\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\hat{w}}}(C_{0})|=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(C_{0})
, we see that
B_{0}
and
C_{0}
have the same core and the same weight, and hence
B_{0}=C_{0}
.
Consequently,
B
and
C
lie in the same
\mathbf{W}_{e}
-orbit.
∎

Recall the function
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{s}}
in Definition
2.2
.

Definition 3.14
.

Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, and let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
. Define

\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}:=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{s}}(\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w}|}(\mathsf{core}(B)))).

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
be lying in a block
B
of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, and let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
.

(1)

We have
\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*};\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B})
for some
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}\in\mathcal{P}^{e}
.

(2)

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}\in\mathcal{P}^{e}
be that satisfying part (1). If
j\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
, then

\iota_{\ell,e}((\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}};\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\circ}}\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}).

(Recall that
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}
denotes the projection of
\mathsf{s}_{j}
onto
\mathfrak{S}_{e}
.)

In particular,
\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\circ}}\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}
.

Proof.

(1)

We have
\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w}|}(\mathsf{core}(B))
, so that

\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w}))=\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w}|}(\mathsf{core}(B)))=\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}}(\boldsymbol{\varnothing}).

Now apply Lemma
3.1
(1).

(2)

By part (1),
\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})))=\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})
.
Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\bullet}}\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w}))=\mathbf{U}(\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\bullet}}\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w}))=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}^{w}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w}))=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}((\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda})^{w}))

by (
2.6
), (
2.7
) and Lemma
2.8
(2) respectively.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}((\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda})^{w})))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\bullet}}\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})))=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\circ}}\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})))

\displaystyle=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\circ}}\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})=\beta_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\smash{\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\circ}}}\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}}((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}})

by (
2.8
) and Lemma
2.8
(1). Part (2) thus follows.

The last assertion now follows from part (1), since
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lies in
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
.

∎

3.2.
Core blocks

The notion of core blocks was first introduced by Fayers in
[
Fay07
]
.
These are blocks where all multipartitions lying in it are multicores, and are believed to be the most elementary non-simple blocks of Ariki-Koike algebras.

We first note the following lemma:

Lemma 3.16
.

The set of core blocks of Ariki-Koike algebras (associated with the same
\ell
-charge) is invariant under the left action of
\mathbf{W}_{e}
.

Proof.

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\lambda^{(1)},\dotsc,\lambda^{(\ell)})\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
.
Then
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{r_{1}}{\bullet}}\lambda^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{r_{\ell}}{\bullet}}\lambda^{(\ell)})
for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
,
where
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{r_{i}}{\bullet}}\lambda^{(i)}
is the partition obtained from
\lambda^{(i)}
by adding all its addable nodes of
(e,r_{i})
-residue
j
and removing all its removable nodes of
(e,r_{i})
-residue
j
.
It is well known that adding all addable nodes of
(e,t)
-residue
j
and removing all removable nodes of
(e,t)
-residue
j
from a partition preserves the
e
-weight of the partition, for all
j\in[1,\,e]
and
t\in\mathbb{Z}
. (Alternatively, one can also use Lemma
3.9
(2).)
Thus
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
is a multicore if and only if
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}
is.
∎

Theorem 3.17
.

Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, and let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
. Then
B
is a core block if and only if
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)=0
for some
i\in[1,\,\ell]
.

Proof.

Let
w=\mathbf{u}\sigma
where
\mathbf{u}=(u_{1},\dotsc,u_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}
.
Then for any
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\lambda^{(1)},\dotsc,\lambda^{(\ell)})\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
, we have
(\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w}=((\lambda^{(\sigma(1))},\dotsc,\lambda^{(\sigma(\ell))});(r_{\sigma(1)}+eu_{\sigma(1)},\dotsc,r_{\sigma(\ell)}+eu_{\sigma(\ell)}))
.

Suppose that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lies in
B
with
\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\beta_{r_{i}}(\lambda^{(i)}))>0
for some
i
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w}=\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu^{(1)},\dotsc,\mu^{(\ell)})
and let
\mathbf{r}^{w}=(r^{\prime}_{1},\dotsc,r^{\prime}_{\ell})
.
Then

\lambda^{(i)}=\mu^{(a)}
and
r_{i}+u_{i}e=r^{\prime}_{a}
, where
a=\sigma^{-1}(i)
, and
so

\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\beta_{r^{\prime}_{a}}(\mu^{(a)}))=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\beta_{r_{i}+u_{i}e}(\lambda^{(i)}))=\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\beta_{r_{i}}(\lambda^{(i)}))^{+u_{i}e})=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\beta_{r_{i}}(\lambda^{(i)}))>0.

Thus
there exists an
x\in\beta_{r^{\prime}_{a}}(\mu^{(a)})
such that
x-e\notin\beta_{r^{\prime}_{a}}(\mu^{(a)})
.
Consequently,
\upsilon_{a}(x)\in\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}))
and
\upsilon_{a}(x)-e\ell=\upsilon_{a}(x-e)\notin\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}))
.
Hence all components of
\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r}^{w})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
would be positive.

Conversely, suppose that
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)>0
for all
i\in[1,\,\ell]
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
be lying in
B
, and let
\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{\mathbf{r}^{w}})=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}))
.
Since
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{t})^{\mathbf{r}^{w}})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
,
if
\mathbf{r}^{w}=(r^{\prime}_{1},\dotsc,r^{\prime}_{\ell})
and
\mathbf{t}^{*}=(t^{*}_{1},\dotsc,t^{*}_{\ell})

then

r^{\prime}_{i}=t^{*}_{i}+\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)-\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i-1}(B)
for all
i\in[1,\,\ell]
by Corollary
3.5
, where
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{0}(B):=\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{\ell}(B)
.
By Corollary
3.8
,
there exists
\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu^{(1)},\dotsc,\mu^{(\ell)})\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
such that
\mathsf{core}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r}^{w})=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}))
,
\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r}^{w})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
and
\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mu^{(i)})\geq\mathrm{min}\{\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i^{\prime}}(B)\mid i^{\prime}\in[1,\,\ell]\}>0
for some
i\in[1,\ell]
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\nu}:=\boldsymbol{\mu}^{{w}^{-1}}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\nu};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathsf{core}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r}^{w})=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}^{*}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*}))=\mathsf{core}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w});

\displaystyle\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\nu};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r}^{w})=\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)=\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w}).

In particular
\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\nu};\mathbf{r})^{w})=|\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\nu};\mathbf{r})^{w})|=|\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})|=\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
.
Thus
\mathsf{core}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})=\mathsf{core}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
and
\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\nu})=\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
, so that
\boldsymbol{\nu}
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lie in the same block, namely
B
.
If
\boldsymbol{\nu}=(\nu^{(1)},\dotsc,\nu^{(\ell)})
, then
\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\nu^{(\overline{w}(i))})=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mu^{(i)})>0
, so that
B
is not a core block.
∎

We end this subsection with some necessary and sufficient conditions, in terms of moving vectors, for a block to be a core block and for a partition to lie in a core block, which follow immediately from Theorem
3.17
and Lemma
3.7
.

Corollary 3.18
.

(1)

Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(a)

B
is a core block.

(b)

For all
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
,
\mathrm{mv}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)=0
for some
i\in[1,\,\ell]
.

(c)

For all
i\in[1,\,\ell]
, there exists
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
such that
\mathrm{mv}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)=0
.

(d)

There exists
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
such that
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}_{\ell}(B)=0
.

(2)

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}(n)
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(a)

\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lies in a core block.

(b)

For all
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
,
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
has a zero component.

(c)

For all
i\in[1,\,\ell]
, there exists
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
and the
i
-th component of
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
equals zero.

(d)

There exists
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
and the last component of
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
equals zero.

3.3.
Weight graphs of multipartitions lying in a core block

In
[
LR16
, para. after Proposition 2.6]
, Lyle and Ruff define the
weight graph

G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
for
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=(\lambda^{(1)},\dotsc,\lambda^{(\ell)})
lying in a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
as follows:

G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
has vertex set
\{1,\dotsc,\ell\}
and no loops, and the number of (undirected) edges between
i
and
j
(
i\neq j
) equals the
\mathcal{H}^{ij}
-weight
\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}^{ij}}(\lambda^{(i)},\lambda^{(j)})
of the bipartition
(\lambda^{(i)},\lambda^{(j)})
in the Ariki-Koike algebra
\mathcal{H}^{ij}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{F},q,(r_{i},r_{j})}(|\lambda^{(i)}|+|\lambda^{(j)}|)
.
It follows from
[
Fay07
, Subsection 3.5]
that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
is ‘indecomposable’ if and only if
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
is connected, and that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
can be decomposed into constituents naturally associated with the connected components of
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.
Furthermore,
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
and
G(\boldsymbol{\mu})
have the same connected components when
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
and
\boldsymbol{\mu}
lie in the same block.

In this subsection, we define a graph
\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)
that is naturally associated to the moving vector
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)
of a core block
B
of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
(where
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
), and show that there is a natural bijective correspondence between its connected components and those of
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
for any
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
.

Definition 3.19
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
and let
\mathbf{r}^{\prime}\in\mathbf{r}^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}}\cap\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
. Define a undirected graph
\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B)
as follows: Its vertex set
V(\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}(B))=\{1,\dotsc,\ell\}
and there is an edge between
i
and
j
if and only if
j\equiv_{\ell}i+1
and
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{\prime}}_{i}(B)\neq 0
.

We begin with a lemma to understand the situation when there is an edge between two vertices in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.

Lemma 3.20
.

Let
(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})\in\mathcal{B}^{\ell}
.
Let
i\in[1,\,\ell]
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, and write
k=a\ell+b
, where
a\in\mathbb{Z}
and
b\in[1-i,\,\ell-i]
.
Let
y\in\mathbb{Z}
, and recall the maps
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}=\upsilon_{i}
and
\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}=\mathbf{U}
in Subsection
2.5
.

(1)

We have

\displaystyle\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(y)-ke

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{e,\ell,i+b}(y-ae),

\displaystyle\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y)-(2a+1)e

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y-ae),

\displaystyle\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y)-(2a-1)e

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y-ae).

(2)

The following statements are equivalent:

(a)

\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(y)-ke\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
.

(b)

y-ae\notin\mathsf{B}_{i+b}
.

(c)

\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y)-(2a+1)e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)})
.

(d)

\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y)-(2a-1)e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)},\mathsf{B}^{(i)})
.

(3)

If
y\in\mathsf{B}^{(i)}
and any of the statements in part (2) holds, then

\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(\mathrm{min}\{i,i+b\})},\mathsf{B}^{(\mathrm{max}\{i,i+b\})}))>0.

Proof.

Part (1) follows immediately from the definition of
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}
and Lemma
2.3
(4), and part (2) follows from part (1) and the definition of
\mathbf{U}_{e,2}
and
\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}
.

For part (3), if
i<i+b
, then
b>0
, and so
a\geq 0
since
k=a\ell+b>0
. Thus

\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)})\ni\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y)>\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y)-(2a+1)e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)})

and hence
\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}_{(i+b)}))>0
.
On the other hand, if
i\leq i+b
, then
b\leq 0
, so that
a>0
.
Thus

\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)},\mathsf{B}^{(i)})\ni\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y)>\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y)-(2a-1)e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)},\mathsf{B}^{(i)}),

and hence
\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i+b)},\mathsf{B}^{(i)}))>0
.
∎

Theorem 3.21
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, and let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
. Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
be lying in
B
, and let
i,j\in\{1,\dotsc,\ell\}
with
i\neq j
.
Then
i
and
j
are connected in
\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
if and only if
\overline{w}(i)
and
\overline{w}(j)
are connected in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.
(Recall that
\overline{w}
denotes the projection of
w
onto
\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}
.)

Proof.

Let
\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})=(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
.
Let
w=\mathbf{t}\sigma
where
\mathbf{t}=(t_{1},\dotsc,t_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
and
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{\ell}
.
Then
\overline{w}=\sigma
and

\mathsf{B}^{(a)}=\beta_{r_{\sigma(a)}+et_{\sigma(a)}}(\lambda^{(\sigma(a))})

for all
a\in[1,\,\ell]
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
i<j
.

Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}^{ij}=(r_{\sigma(i)},r_{\sigma(j)}),\qquad\mathbf{t}^{ij}=(t_{\sigma(i)},t_{\sigma(j)}),\qquad\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij}

\displaystyle=(\lambda^{(\sigma(i))},\lambda^{(\sigma(j))}).

Then

\beta_{(\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\mathbf{t}^{ij}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij})=(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)}).

Since
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{s}}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{s}}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w}))=\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
, we see that
(\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\mathbf{t}^{ij}}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{s}}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)})\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{2}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)}))=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\beta_{(\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\mathbf{t}^{ij}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij})))=\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij};\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\mathbf{t}^{ij}})=\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}^{\sigma(i),\sigma(j)}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij}).

(3.1)

Since
(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ji};\mathbf{r}^{ji})=(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij};\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\mathsf{s}_{1}}\in(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij};\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{2}}
, we have

\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}^{\sigma(j),\sigma(i)}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ji})=\mathrm{min}(\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ji};\mathbf{r}^{ji})^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{2}}))=\mathrm{min}(\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij};\mathbf{r}^{ij})^{\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{2}}))=\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}^{\sigma(i),\sigma(j)}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{ij}).

(3.2)

Suppose that
\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}^{\sigma(i),\sigma(j)}}(\lambda^{(\sigma(i))},\lambda^{(\sigma(j))})>0
.
Then
\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)}))>0
.
Thus there exists
x\in\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)})=\upsilon_{e,2,1}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)})\cup\upsilon_{e,2,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(j)})
such that
x-e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)})
.

If
x\in\upsilon_{e,2,1}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)})
, say
x=\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y)
where
y\in\mathsf{B}^{(i)}
,
then
\upsilon_{e,2,1}(y)-e=x-e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)})
, so that

\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(y)\in\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)})\subseteq\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
and
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(y)-(j-i)e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
by Lemma
3.20
(with
k=j-i
,
a=0
and
b=j-i
).
Consequently,
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B),\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i+1}(B),\dotsc,\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{j-1}(B)>0
so that
i
is connected to
j
in
\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
.

On the other hand, if
x\in\upsilon_{e,2,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(j)})
, say
x=\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y)
where
y\in\mathsf{B}^{(j)}
,
then
\upsilon_{e,2,2}(y)-e=x-e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,2}(\mathsf{B}^{(i)},\mathsf{B}^{(j)})
, so that

\upsilon_{e,\ell,j}(y)\in\upsilon_{e,\ell,j}(\mathsf{B}^{(j)})\subseteq\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
and
\upsilon_{e,\ell,j}(y)-(\ell-j+i)e\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
by Lemma
3.20
(with
k=\ell-j+i
,
a=1
and
b=i-j
).
Consequently,

\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{j}(B),\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{j+1}(B),\dotsc,\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{\ell}(B),\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{1}(B),\dotsc,\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i-1}(B)>0

so that
j
is connected to
i
in
\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
.

Now suppose that
\mathrm{mv}_{i}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)>0
, and let
i^{+}\in[1,\,\ell]
be such that
i^{+}\equiv_{\ell}i+1
.
Then the
i
-th component of
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
is positive.
Thus there exists
x\in\mathbb{Z}
such that

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle:=\{c\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\mid\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(x)+ce\in\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})\},

\displaystyle D

\displaystyle:=\{d\in\mathbb{Z}^{+}\mid\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(x)-de\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})\}

are both nonempty.
Let
c_{\mathrm{min}}:=\mathrm{min}(C)
and
d_{\mathrm{min}}:=\mathrm{min}(D)
,
and let
y_{C},y_{D}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
i_{C},i_{D}\in[1,\,\ell]
be such that

\displaystyle\upsilon_{e,\ell,i_{C}}(y_{C})

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(x)+c_{\mathrm{min}}e,

\displaystyle\upsilon_{e,\ell,i_{D}}(y_{D})

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(x)-d_{\mathrm{min}}e.

Then
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i_{C}}(y_{C})\in\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
, so that
y_{C}\in\mathsf{B}_{i_{C}}
,
while
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i_{D}}(y_{D})\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
.
Consequently,
\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}^{\sigma(i_{C}),\sigma(i_{D})}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{i_{C},i_{D}})>0
by Lemma
3.20
(3), (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), so that
\sigma(i_{C})
and
\sigma(i_{D})
are connected in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.

If
c_{\mathrm{min}}=0
, then
i=i_{C}
and trivially
\sigma(i)
is connected to
\sigma(i_{C})
in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.
On the other hand, if
c_{\mathrm{min}}>0
, then
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(x)\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
. Since
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i_{C}}(y_{C})\in\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
, by the same argument used in the last paragraph,
\sigma(i)
and
\sigma(i_{C})
are connected in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.

If
d_{\mathrm{min}}=1
, then
i_{D}=i^{+}
. Thus,
\sigma(i^{+})
is connected to
\sigma(i_{D})
trivially.
On the other hand, if
d_{\mathrm{min}}>1
, then
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i^{+}}(x)=\upsilon_{e,\ell,i}(x)-e\in\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
. Since
\upsilon_{e,\ell,i_{D}}(y_{D})\notin\mathbf{U}_{e,\ell}(\mathsf{B}^{(1)},\dotsc,\mathsf{B}^{(\ell)})
, we see that
\sigma(i^{+})
and and
\sigma(i_{D})
are connected in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
by the same argument.

Thus,
\sigma(i)
,
\sigma(i_{C})
,
\sigma(i_{D})
and
\sigma(i^{+})
all lie in the same connected component of
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
, so that
\sigma(i)
and
\sigma(i^{+})
are connected in
G(\boldsymbol{\lambda})
.
∎

Since
\Gamma_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(B)
is disconnected if and only if there exist distinct
i,j\in[1,\,\ell]
such that
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)=\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{j}(B)=0
, we get the following immediate corollary, where the equivalence of the second and third statements follows from Lemma
3.10
(1).

Corollary 3.22
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
. For each
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
, let
\mathbf{mv}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=(m^{w}_{1},\dotsc,m^{w}_{\ell})
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

\boldsymbol{\lambda}
is decomposable (in the sense of Fayers).

(2)

There exist
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
and distinct
i,j\in[1,\,\ell]
such that
m^{w}_{i}=m^{w}_{j}=0
.

(3)

For all
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
, there exist distinct
i,j\in[1,\,\ell]
such that
m^{w}_{i}=m^{w}_{j}=0
.

4.
Scopes equivalence between core blocks

Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
.
Following
[
Lyl23b
,
Web24
]
, we say that
B
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
are
Scopes
equivalent
if all
\ell
-partitions lying in
B
have no addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
j
or all
\ell
-partitions lying in
B
have no removable node with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
j
. Clearly, Scopes equivalence is symmetric by definition, and we further extend it to
an equivalence relation on all blocks by taking its reflexive and transitive closure.

In this section, we obtain a necessary and sufficient condition for two core blocks to be Scopes equivalent.

The importance of Scopes equivalence is due to the following theorem which is well known among experts.

Theorem 4.1

(see
[
CR08
, Theorem 6.4]
and
[
Web24
, Lemma 3.1]
)
.

Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
and
j\in{\mathbb{Z}}/e{\mathbb{Z}}
. Suppose that for all
\ell
-partitions
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lying in
B
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
has no addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
j
. Then
B
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
are Morita equivalent. Under this equivalence, the Specht module
S^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
lying in
B
corresponds to
S^{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
.

Recall
\mathbf{r}_{B}^{w*}
for a block
B
and
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
in Definition
3.14
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
.
Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
with
\mathbf{r}_{B}^{w*}=(x^{B}_{1},\dotsc,x^{B}_{e})
.
If
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)=0
, then
for any
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
with
\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})))=(\mathsf{C}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{C}_{e})
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})+i}{\ell}<\left\lceil\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rceil\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})+i}{\ell}\geq\left\lfloor\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rfloor

for all
j\in[1,\,e]
.

Proof.

If
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)=0
, then for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
,
\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
has no node with
(\ell,\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B})
-residue
\ell-i
.
Now,
\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
will have a node with
(\ell,\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B})
-residue
\ell-i
if
there exist
x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
x<y\leq z
,
x\notin\mathsf{C}_{j}
,
y\equiv_{\ell}\ell-i
and
z\in\mathsf{C}_{j}
for some
j\in[1,\,e]
.

Since
\mathcal{s}(\mathsf{C}_{j})=x^{B}_{j}
, we have
\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})\geq x^{B}_{j}
if and only if
\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})<x^{B}_{j}
.
Thus
if
\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})\geq\left\lceil\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rceil\ell-i
,
then
\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})\geq x^{B}_{j}
and hence
\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})<x^{B}_{j}
.
Consequently,

\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})<x^{B}_{j}\leq\left\lceil\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rceil\ell-i\leq\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j}).

On the other hand, if
\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})<\left\lfloor\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rfloor\ell-i
, then
\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})<x^{B}_{j}
and hence
\mathrm{max}(C_{j})\geq x^{B}_{j}
.
Thus

\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})<\left\lfloor\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rfloor\ell-i\leq x^{B}_{j}\leq\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j}).

This shows that in either of these cases, we have found
x,y,z\in\mathbb{Z}
with
x=\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j})\notin\mathsf{C}_{j}
,
y\in\left\{\left\lceil\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rceil\ell-i,\left\lfloor\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rfloor\ell-i\right\}
and
z=\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})\in\mathsf{C}_{j}
,
so that
\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})
has a node with
(\ell,\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B})
-residue
\ell-i
.
∎

Corollary 4.3
.

The weight of a core block of an Ariki-Koike algebra is bounded above by

\left\lfloor\ell/2\right\rfloor\left\lceil\ell/2\right\rceil e.

Proof.

Let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
.
Let
B
be a core block with
\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}=(x^{B}_{1},\dotsc,x^{B}_{e})
.
By Theorem
3.17
, there exists
i\in[1,\ell]
such that
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)=0
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
be lying in
B
, and
let
\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})))=(\mathsf{C}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{C}_{e})
.
By Lemma
4.2
,
we have
\mathsf{C}_{j}=\mathbb{Z}_{<m}\cup L_{j}
for some
L_{j}\subseteq[m,\,m+\ell-1]
, where
m=\lfloor\frac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\rfloor\ell-i
,
so that
|\beta^{-1}(\mathsf{C}_{j})|\leq|L_{j}|(\ell-|L_{j}|)\leq\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor\lceil\ell/2\rceil
.
Consequently,

\mathsf{wt}(B)=\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda})=\mathsf{wt}_{e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w})=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w})))=\sum_{j=1}^{e}|\beta^{-1}(\mathsf{C}_{j})|\leq\lfloor\ell/2\rfloor\lceil\ell/2\rceil e.

∎

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
w\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
.
Let
B
be a block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
with
\mathbf{r}^{w*}_{B}=(x^{B}_{1},\dotsc,x^{B}_{e})
.
If there exist
i\in[1,\,\ell]
and
j\in[1,\,e]
such that:

•

x^{B}_{j+1}>x^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}\ell
;

•

\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w}}_{i}(B)=0
;

•

there exists
x\in[x^{B}_{j},\,x^{B}_{j+1}-\delta_{je}\ell]
such that
\ell\mid(x+i)
,

then for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
does not have any addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
;
in particular,
B
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
are Scopes equivalent.

Here,
j+1
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}
are to be read as
1
and
\mathsf{s}_{0}
respectively when
j=e
.

Proof.

Let
x+i=k\ell
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
be lying in
B
, and let
\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{\mathbf{r}}}}(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w_{\mathbf{r}}})))=(\mathsf{C}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{C}_{e})
.
For each
j\in[1,\,e]
and
b\in\mathsf{C}_{j}
, we have, by Lemma
4.2
,

b\leq\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})<\left\lceil\tfrac{x^{B}_{j}+i}{\ell}\right\rceil\ell-i\leq\left\lceil\frac{x+i}{\ell}\right\rceil\ell-i=k\ell-i\leq\left\lfloor\tfrac{x^{B}_{j+1}-\delta_{je}\ell+i}{\ell}\right\rfloor\ell-i\leq\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j+1})-\delta_{je}\ell.

Thus
b\in\mathsf{C}_{j+1}
if
j\neq e
and
b+\ell\in\mathsf{C}_{j+1}
if
j=e
.
This shows that
\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{w}
does not have any addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r}^{w})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
, and hence
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
does not have any addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
.
∎

For the remainder of this section, we shall be working towards providing a necessary and sufficient conditions for two core blocks of Ariki-Koike algebras (with common
\ell
-charge
\mathbf{r}
) to be Scopes equivalent.
As two Scopes equivalent blocks must lie in the same
\mathbf{W}_{e}
-orbit, they have the same moving vectors by
Proposition
3.13
.
As such, we shall fix a
w_{0}\in\widehat{\mathbf{W}}_{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
and
\mathrm{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}_{\ell}(B)=0
for all such core blocks considered, and there is no loss of generality in doing so by Corollary
3.18
(1).
We can then simplify our notations and write
\mathbf{mv}(B)
for
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(B)
, and
\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}
for
\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}*}_{B}
.

We shall also fix
x^{B}_{1},\dotsc,x^{B}_{e}\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}=(x^{B}_{1},\dotsc,x^{B}_{e})
.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
Define

\mathbf{y}^{B}=(y^{B}_{1},\dotsc,y^{B}_{e})\in\mathbb{Z}^{e}
and
\mathbf{z}^{B}=(z^{B}_{1},\dotsc,z^{B}_{e})\in(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})^{e}
by

\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}=\mathbf{y}^{B}\ell+\mathbf{z}^{B}.

Furthermore, define
\mathfrak{y}_{B}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
j_{B}\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
by

|\mathbf{y}^{B}|=\mathfrak{y}_{B}e+j_{B}.

Example 4.6
.

Let
e=5
,
\ell=4
,
\mathbf{r}=(1,3,3,6)
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=((3,2,1^{4}),(4,2,1),(2^{2},1),(1))
.
We have seen in Example
3.3
that
\iota_{\ell,e}(\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})=(((1),(1),\varnothing,\varnothing,\varnothing);(0,6,1,4,2))
, and
\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})=(0,1,0,1)
.
Since
\mathbf{r}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
, we see that the block
B
in which
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lies is a core block, with
\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}}(B)=(0,1,0,1)
.

We choose
w_{0}=\rho_{4}\mathbf{e}_{4}
. Then

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}}

\displaystyle=(((4,2,1),(2^{2},1),(1),(3,2,1^{4})),(3,3,6,6)),

\displaystyle\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})

\displaystyle=(((1),(1),\varnothing,\varnothing,\varnothing);(1,7,2,5,3)),

\displaystyle\mathbf{mv}^{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(B)

\displaystyle=(1,0,1,0).

Thus
\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}=(1,7,2,5,3)
,
\mathbf{y}^{B}=(0,1,0,1,0)
,
\mathbf{z}^{B}=(1,3,2,1,3)
,
\mathfrak{y}_{B}=0
and
j_{B}=2
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
For each
j\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\circ}}\mathbf{y}^{B}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbf{z}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{0}{\circ}}\mathbf{z}^{B}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}}.

In particular,
|\mathbf{y}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}|=|\mathbf{y}^{B}|
, and so
\mathfrak{y}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\mathfrak{y}_{B}
and
j_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=j_{B}
.
(Recall that
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}
denotes the projection of
\mathsf{s}_{j}
onto
\mathfrak{S}_{e}
.)

Proof.

By Lemma
3.15
(2),
\mathbf{r}^{*}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\ell}{\circ}}\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}
. The lemma thus follows directly from Definition
4.5
.
∎

The following is a reformulation of Corollary
4.4
.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
Let
j\in[1,\,e]
.
Suppose that one of the following holds:

•

y^{B}_{j+1}>y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
;

•

y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
,
z^{B}_{j+1}>z^{B}_{j}
and there exists
i\in[1,\,\ell]
with
\mathrm{mv}_{i}(B)=0
such that

z^{B}_{j+1}\geq\ell-i\geq z^{B}_{j}
.

Then
\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)>0
, and for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
,
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
has no addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
.
In particular,
B
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
are Scopes equivalent.

Here,
j+1
,
\mathsf{s}_{j}
and
\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)
are to be read as
1
,
\mathsf{s}_{0}
and
\mathsf{hub}_{0}(B)
respectively when
j=e
.

Proof.

Note first that
\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)=x^{B}_{j+1}-x^{B}_{j}-\delta_{je}\ell
, so that
\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)>0
if and only if
x^{B}_{j+1}>x^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}\ell
, which is equivalent to

y^{B}_{j+1}>y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}\qquad\text{or}\qquad(y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}\text{ and }z^{B}_{j+1}>z^{B}_{j}).

If
y^{B}_{j+1}>y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
, then

x^{B}_{j}=y^{B}_{j}\ell+z^{B}_{j}<(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell\leq y^{B}_{j+1}\ell-\delta_{je}\ell\leq x^{B}_{j+1}-\delta_{je}\ell,

so that
x=(y^{B}_{j+1}+1)\ell
and
i=\ell
satisfy the conditions in Corollary
4.4
.

On the other hand, if
y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
,
z^{B}_{j+1}>z^{B}_{j}
, and
\mathrm{mv}_{i}(B)=0
with
z^{B}_{j}\leq\ell-i\leq z^{B}_{j+1}
, then

x^{B}_{j}=y^{B}_{j}\ell+z^{B}_{j}\leq y^{B}_{j}\ell+(\ell-i)\leq y^{B}_{j}\ell+z^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j+1}\ell-\delta_{je}\ell+z^{B}_{j+1}=x^{B}_{j+1}-\delta_{je}\ell.

Thus the conditions in Corollary
4.4
are satisfied with
x=y^{B}_{j}\ell+(\ell-i)
.

Consequently, the Lemma follows from Corollary
4.4
.
∎

Definition 4.9
.

(1)

For each
\mathbf{y}=(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{e})\in\mathbb{Z}^{e}
, define
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{e}
by: for each
j\in[1,\,e-1]
,

y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(j)}\leq y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(j+1)},

with equality only if
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(j)<\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(j+1)
.
(In other words,
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}
is the element of
\mathfrak{S}_{e}
with minimal length such that
\mathbf{y}^{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}}
is weakly increasing.)

(2)

Let
I\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
0\in I
. For
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, define

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(b):=\mathrm{max}\{i\in I\mid b\geq i\}.

Furthermore, for
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\dotsc,z_{e})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{e}
, define

\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathfrak{S}_{e}
by: for each
j\in[1,\,e-1]
,

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)}),

with equality only if one of the following holds:

•

z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)}>z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)})
;

•

\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)<\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)
and (
z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)}=z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)}
or

z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)},z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)}>\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j+1)})
).

(It is an easy exercise to show that
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}
is unique/well-defined.)

Example 4.10
.

Let
\mathbf{y}=(0,1,0,1,0)
. Then
\mathbf{y}^{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}}=(0,0,0,1,1)
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}=\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{s}_{4}\mathsf{s}_{3}
.

Let
\mathbf{z}=(1,2,3,3,1)
, and
I=\{0,2\}
. Then
\mathbf{z}^{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}}=(3,3,2,1,1)
and
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}=\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathsf{s}_{3}\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{s}_{3}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\dotsc,z_{e})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{e}
and
I\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
0\in I
.
If
j,j^{\prime}\in[1,\,e]
are such that
z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)}>z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)})\geq z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}
, then
j<j^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Since
z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)}>z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}
, we see that
j\neq j^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})})
by the definition of
\mathsf{ht}_{I}
.
If
j>j^{\prime}
, then by the definition of
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}
, we have

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime}+1)})\geq\dotsb\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j-1)})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)}).

Combining with the inequality
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})})
obtained above, we get

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime}+1)})=\dotsb=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j-1)})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)}).

Now, the condition
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)})\geq z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}
implies that

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j)})\geq z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}),

forcing
z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(j^{\prime})})
.
Let
m=\mathrm{max}\{b\in[j^{\prime},j-1]\mid z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)})\}
.
Then

z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(m)}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(m)})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(m+1)})<z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(m+1)},

contradicting the definition of
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}
. Hence,
j<j^{\prime}
.
∎

Corollary 4.12
.

Let
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\dotsc,z_{e})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{e}
and
I\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
0\in I
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}=1_{\mathfrak{S}_{e}}
.

(2)

Whenever
b,b^{\prime}\in[1,\,e]
such that
z_{b}>z_{b^{\prime}}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})\geq z_{b^{\prime}}
, we have
b<b^{\prime}
.

(3)

There does not exist
b\in[1,\,e-1]
such that
z_{b+1}>z_{b}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})\geq z_{b}
.

(4)

For all
b\in[1,\,e-1]
, we have
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})
,
and if
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})\in\{z_{b},z_{b+1}\}
, then
z_{b}\geq z_{b+1}
.

Proof.

That
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
follows from Lemma
4.11
, while
(2)\Rightarrow(3)
is trivial.

If
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})<\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})
, then
z_{b}<\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})\leq z_{b+1}
by the definition of
\mathsf{ht}_{I}
, so that (3) does not hold.
Also, if
z_{b}<z_{b+1}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})\in\{z_{b},z_{b+1}\}
, then

\{z_{b},z_{b+1}\}\ni\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})\leq z_{b}<z_{b+1},

so that
z_{b}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{b+1})<z_{b+1}
, and once again (3) does not hold.
Thus
(3)\Rightarrow(4)
.

If (4) holds, then it is straightforward to verify that
1_{\mathfrak{S}_{e}}
satisfies the defining condition for
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}
, so that
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}=1_{\mathfrak{S}_{e}}
, i.e. (1) holds.
∎

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
j\in[1,\,e]
.

(1)

Let
\mathbf{y}=(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{e})\in\mathbb{Z}^{e}
. If
y_{j+1}>y_{j}+\delta_{je}
, then

\sigma_{\smash[t]{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\circ}}\mathbf{y}}}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}.

(2)

Let
\mathbf{z}=(z_{1},\dotsc,z_{e})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{e}
and
I\subseteq\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
with
0\in I
.
If
j<e
,
z_{j+1}>z_{j}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j+1})\geq z_{j}
,
then

\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}}}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}.

Again,
j+1
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}
are to be read as
1
and
\mathsf{s}_{0}
respectively when
j=e
.

Proof.

(1)

Let
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}=(y^{\prime}_{1},\dotsc,y^{\prime}_{e}):=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\circ}}\mathbf{y}
,
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(a)=j
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(b)=j+1
.
Then

\displaystyle y^{\prime}_{c}=\begin{cases}y_{j+1}-\delta_{je},&\text{if }c=j;\\
y_{j}+\delta_{je},&\text{if }c=j+1;\\
y_{c},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

\displaystyle y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(a)}=y_{j}<y_{j+1}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(b)},

so that
a<b
.
To show part (1), we show that
y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}\leq y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}
, with equality only if
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)<\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)
for all
c\in[1,\,e-1]
. We do this by considering six cases separately.

Case 1.
\{c,c+1\}\cap\{a,b\}=\emptyset
:

this follows directly from the properties of
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}
.

Case 2.
c+1=a
:

Since
a<b
, we have
c\notin\{a,b\}
, or equivalently,
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)\notin\{j,j+1\}
. Thus

y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}\leq y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(a)}=y_{j}=y^{\prime}_{j+1}-\delta_{je}\leq y^{\prime}_{j+1}=y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(a)}=y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)},

with equality only if
y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}
and
\delta_{je}=0
, which yields
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)<\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)=j<e
, in which case
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)<\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)
.

Case 3.
c=b
:

This is similar to Case 2, and we omit the details here.

Case 4.
c=a
and
c+1\neq b
:

By the property of
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}
, we have
either
y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}
and
\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)<\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)
— in which case,
j=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)<e
and so
\delta_{je}=0
— or
y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}\leq y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}-1
.
In the former case, we get

y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y^{\prime}_{j+1}=y_{j}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}=y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}

and
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)=j+1<\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)
.
In the latter case, we have

y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y^{\prime}_{j+1}=y_{j}+\delta_{je}=y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}+\delta_{je}\leq y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}-1+\delta_{je}\leq y_{\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}=y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)},

with equality only if
j=e
in which case
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)=1<\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)
.

Case 5.
c\neq a
and
c+1=b
:

This is similar to Case 4, and we omit the details here.

Case 6.
c=a
and
c+1=b
:

We have

y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}=y^{\prime}_{j+1}=y_{j}+\delta_{je}<y_{j+1}=y^{\prime}_{j}+\delta_{je}.

Thus

y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)}\leq y^{\prime}_{j}+\delta_{je}-1\leq y^{\prime}_{j}=y^{\prime}_{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)}

with equality only if
j=e
, which case
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c)=1<e=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}}(c+1)
.

(2)

Let
(z^{\prime}_{1},\dotsc,z^{\prime}_{e})=\mathbf{z}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}}
. Then
z^{\prime}_{b}=z_{\mathsf{s}_{j}(b)}
for all
b\in[1,\,e]
.
Thus for
b\in[1,\,e-1]
, we have

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)})\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}),

with equality only if one of the following holds:

•

z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)}>z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}=\mathsf{ht}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)})
;

•

\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)<\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)
and (
z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)}=z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}
or

z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)},z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}>\mathsf{ht}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)})
).

These conditions can easily be seen to be equivalent to the following, except when
\{j,j+1\}=\{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b),\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)\}
:

•

z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)}>z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}=\mathsf{ht}(z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)})
;

•

\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)<\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)
and (
z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)}=z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}
or

z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)},z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}>\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)})
).

Now if
\{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b),\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)\}=\{j,j+1\}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)})
, then
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j+1})
.
Since we are given that
z_{j+1}>z_{j}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j+1})\geq z_{j}
, this implies that

\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j+1})\geq z_{j}\geq\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j+1}),

forcing equality throughout, yielding

z_{j+1}>z_{j}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j+1}).

Consequently none of the conditions for
\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)})=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)})
holds when
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)=j
and
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)=j+1
.
Thus
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)=j+1
and
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)=j
, and so
z_{j+1}>z_{j}=\mathsf{ht}_{I}(z_{j})
yields

z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b)}>z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}=\mathsf{ht}(z^{\prime}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}(b+1)}).

Hence
\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}
satisfies the conditions for
\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}}}
so that

\mathsf{s}_{j}\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}}=\tau^{I}_{\mathbf{z}^{\mathsf{s}_{j}}}
as desired.

∎

Definition 4.14
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. Recall
\mathbf{y}^{B}=(y^{B}_{1},\dotsc,y^{B}_{e})
and
\mathbf{z}^{B}=(z^{B}_{1},\dotsc,z^{B}_{e})
in Definition
4.5
, and let

I_{B}:=\{\ell-i\mid\mathrm{mv}_{i}(B)=0\}.

(1)

Define
\sigma_{B},\tau_{B}\in\mathfrak{S}_{e}
by

\sigma_{B}:=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{B}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau_{B}:=\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}}.

(2)

The
Scopes vector of
B
, denoted
\mathbf{Sc}(B)
, is defined to be

\mathbf{Sc}(B):=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}}=(z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}(1)},\dotsb,z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}(e)}).

(3)

We call
B
an
initial
core block if

(I)

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}\leq y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)}
;

(II)

\tau_{B}=1_{\mathfrak{S}_{e}}
.

Example 4.15
.

Let
e=5
,
\ell=4
,
\mathbf{r}=(1,3,3,6)
, and let
B
be the block of
\mathcal{H}_{22}
containing the
4
-partition
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=((3,2,1^{4}),(4,2,1),(2^{2},1),(1))
.
Let
w_{0}=\rho_{4}\mathbf{e}_{4}
.
Then
\mathbf{mv}(B)=(1,0,1,0)
,
\mathbf{y}^{B}=(0,1,0,1,0)
and
\mathbf{z}^{B}=(1,3,2,1,3)
by Example
4.6
.
Thus
I_{B}=\{0,2\}
.

By Example
4.10
,
\sigma_{B}=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{B}}=\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{s}_{4}\mathsf{s}_{3}
, so that
(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}=(1,2,3,3,1)
.
Thus, by the same example,
\tau_{B}=\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}}=\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{s}_{1}\mathsf{s}_{3}\mathsf{s}_{2}\mathsf{s}_{3}
.
Hence
\mathbf{Sc}(B)=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}}=(3,3,2,1,1)
.

Lemma 4.16
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. If
y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
for some
j\in[1,\,e]
,
then
\sigma_{B}\mathsf{s}_{\sigma_{B}^{-1}(j)}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}
.
(Again,
j+1
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}
are to be read as
1
and
\mathsf{s}_{0}
respectively when
j=e
.)

Proof.

let
\sigma_{B}(a)=j
. If
y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
, then
\sigma_{B}(a+1)=j+1
by the definition of
\sigma_{B}=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{B}}
. Consequently,
\sigma_{B}\mathsf{s}_{a}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}
, as desired.
∎

Proposition 4.17
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
, and let
j\in[1,\,e]
.

(1)

If
y^{B}_{j+1}>y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
, then

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\tau_{B}.

(2)

If
y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
,
z^{B}_{j+1}>z^{B}_{j}
and

\mathsf{ht}(z^{B}_{j+1})\geq z^{B}_{j}
,
then

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\sigma_{B}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=\mathsf{s}_{\sigma_{B}^{-1}(j)}\tau_{B}.

Furthermore, we have
\mathbf{Sc}(\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B)=\mathbf{Sc}(B)
in both of these cases. (Once again,
j+1
and
\mathsf{s}_{j}
are to be read as
1
and
\mathsf{s}_{0}
respectively when
j=e
.)

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B=C
.
Since
\mathbf{mv}(B)=\mathbf{mv}(C)
by Proposition
3.13
, we have
I_{B}=I_{C}
.
Furthermore, we have
\mathbf{y}^{C}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\circ}}\mathbf{y}^{B}
and
\mathbf{z}^{C}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}}
by Lemma
4.7
.

(1)

We have

\sigma_{C}=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{C}}=\sigma_{\smash[t]{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\circ}}\mathbf{y}^{B}}}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{B}}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B},

where the third equality follows from Proposition
4.13
(1).
Thus

\tau_{C}=\tau^{I_{C}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{C})^{\sigma_{C}}}=\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{C}}}=\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}}=\tau_{B}.

Hence,

\mathbf{Sc}(C)=(\mathbf{z}^{C})^{\sigma_{C}\tau_{C}}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{C}\tau_{C}}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}}=\mathbf{Sc}(B).

(2)

Let
\sigma_{B}(a)=j
. Then
\sigma_{B}{\mathsf{s}_{a}}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}
by Lemma
4.16
.
Furthermore, we have

\mathbf{y}^{C}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{1}{\circ}}\mathbf{y}^{B}=\mathbf{y}^{B}
so that
\sigma_{C}=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{C}}=\sigma_{\mathbf{y}^{B}}=\sigma_{B}
.
Thus

\tau_{C}=\tau^{I_{C}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{C})^{\sigma_{C}}}=\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}}}=\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\mathsf{s}_{a}}}=\mathsf{s}_{a}\tau^{I_{B}}_{(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}}=\mathsf{s}_{a}\tau_{B}

where the penultimate equality follows from Proposition
4.13
(2) (note that the
a
-th and
(a+1)
-th component of
(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}
are
z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(a)}=z^{B}_{j}
and
z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(a+1)}=z^{B}_{j+1}
respectively). Now

\mathbf{Sc}(C)=(\mathbf{z}^{C})^{\sigma_{C}\tau_{C}}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{C}\tau_{C}}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}\mathsf{s}_{a}\tau_{B}}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}}=\mathbf{Sc}(B).

∎

Lemma 4.18
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
The following statements are equivalent:

(1)

B
satisfies Condition (I) of being initial, i.e.
y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}\leq y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)}
.

(2)

For all
j\in[1,\,e]
,
y^{B}_{j}=\mathfrak{y}_{B}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq j_{B}}.

(3)

For all
j\in[1,\,e]\setminus\{\overline{j_{B}}\}
,
y^{B}_{\overline{j+1}}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
.

(4)

For all
j\in[1,\,e]
,
y^{B}_{\overline{j+1}}\leq y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
.

(5)

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)}
.

Here, and hereafter, for
x\in\mathbb{Z}
, set
\overline{x}
to be the unique element in
[1,\,e]
satisfying
\overline{x}\equiv_{e}x
.

Proof.

For (1)
\Rightarrow
(2), let
m:=\mathrm{max}\{j\in[1,\,e]\mid y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(j)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}\}
.
Then

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(2)}=\dotsb=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(m)}
, and so
\sigma_{B}(1)<\sigma_{B}(2)<\dotsb<\sigma_{B}(m)
.
If
m=e
, then
y^{B}_{j}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}
for all
j\in[1,\,e]
, so that

\mathfrak{y}_{B}e+j_{B}=|\mathbf{y}^{B}|=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}e
. Hence
y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}=\mathfrak{y}_{B}
and
j_{B}=0
, and so
y^{B}_{j}=\mathfrak{y}_{B}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq j_{B}}
for all
j\in[1,\,e]
.

On the other hand, if
m<e
, then

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(m)}<y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(m+1)}\leq y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(m+2)}\leq\dotsb\leq y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}\leq y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)}\leq y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+1,

forcing

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(m+1)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(m+2)}=\dotsb=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+1.

Hence

\mathfrak{y}_{B}e+j_{B}=|\mathbf{y}^{B}|=my^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+(e-m)(y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+1)=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}e+(e-m)

so that
\mathfrak{y}_{B}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}
and
j_{B}=e-m
.
Furthermore,

\sigma_{B}(m+1)<\sigma_{B}(m+2)<\dotsb<\sigma_{B}(e)
and
\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)
.
We thus have

\sigma_{B}(m+1)<\dotsb<\sigma_{B}(e)<\sigma_{B}(1)<\dotsb<\sigma_{B}(m).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}(m+1)=1,

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}(m+2)=2,

\displaystyle\dotsc,\

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}(e)=e-m,

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}(1)=e-m+1,

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}(2)=e-m+2,

\displaystyle\dotsc,\

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}(m)=e.

Thus
y^{B}_{j}=\mathfrak{y}_{B}+\mathbbm{1}_{j\leq j_{B}}
.

It is straightforward to verify (2)
\Rightarrow
(3)
\Rightarrow
(4), and (5)
\Rightarrow
(1) is trivial.

For (4)
\Rightarrow
(5), we have

y^{B}_{1}\geq\dotsb\geq y^{B}_{e}\geq y^{B}_{1}-1,

by (4).
Let
M=\mathrm{max}\{j\mid y^{B}_{j}=y^{B}_{1}\}
.
Then

y^{B}_{1}=\dotsb=y^{B}_{M}=y^{B}_{M+1}+1=\dotsb=y^{B}_{e}+1.

If
M=e
, then
\sigma_{B}(1)=1
and
\sigma_{B}(e)=e
, and

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}=y^{B}_{e}=y^{B}_{1}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(1)>\sigma_{B}(e)}.

On the other hand, if
M<e
, then
\sigma_{B}(1)=M+1
and
\sigma_{B}(e)=M
,

y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(e)}=y^{B}_{M}=y^{B}_{M+1}+1=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+1=y^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(1)}+\mathbbm{1}_{\sigma_{B}(1)>\sigma_{B}(e)}.

∎

We record the following corollary, which is immediate from Lemma
4.18
(2).

Corollary 4.19
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}

satisfying Condition (I) of being initial.
Then

\displaystyle\sigma_{B}

\displaystyle=\rho_{e}^{j_{B}}=\rho_{e}^{|\mathbf{y}^{B}|};

\displaystyle\mathbf{y}^{B}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|))^{\rho_{e}^{-j_{B}}}=(\smash[t]{\overbrace{\mathfrak{y}+1,\dotsc,\mathfrak{y}+1}^{j_{B}\text{ times}},\overbrace{\mathfrak{y},\dotsc,\mathfrak{y}}^{e-j_{B}\text{ times}}}),

where
\rho_{e}=(1,2,\dotsc,e)\in\mathfrak{S}_{e}
.
(Recall the notation
\mathbf{v}_{e}(x)
in Subsection
2.1
.)

Lemma 4.20
.

Let
B
be an initial core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. Then

\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|)\ell+\mathbf{Sc}(B))^{\rho_{e}^{-j_{B}}}.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.19
,
\mathbf{y}^{B}=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|))^{\sigma_{B}^{-1}}
, and
\sigma_{B}=\rho_{e}^{j_{B}}
.
Since
\mathbf{Sc}(B)=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}}=(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}}
,we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{y}^{B}\ell+\mathbf{z}^{B}=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|))^{\sigma_{B}^{-1}}\ell+(\mathbf{Sc}(B))^{\sigma_{B}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|)\ell+\mathbf{Sc}(B))^{\sigma_{B}^{-1}}=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|)\ell+\mathbf{Sc}(B))^{\rho_{e}^{-j_{B}}}.

∎

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
\mathbf{v}\in[0,\,\ell-1]^{e}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. There is at most one initial core block
B
with
\mathsf{wt}(B)=k
and
\mathbf{Sc}(B)=\mathbf{v}
.

Proof.

Let
B
be an initial core block
B
with
\mathsf{wt}(B)=k
and
\mathbf{Sc}(B)=\mathbf{v}
.
Then
|\mathbf{v}|=|\mathbf{Sc}(B)|=|(\mathbf{z}^{B})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}}|=|\mathbf{z}^{B}|
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{y}_{B}=\frac{|\mathbf{y}^{B}|-j_{B}}{e}=\frac{(|\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}|-|\mathbf{z}^{B}|)/\ell-j_{B}}{e}=\frac{|\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}|-|\mathbf{v}|-j_{B}\,\ell}{e\ell}.

The same would also hold for another initial core block
B^{\prime}
with
\mathsf{wt}(B^{\prime})=k
and
\mathbf{Sc}(B^{\prime})=\mathbf{v}
.
Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}\ni\mathfrak{y}_{B}-\mathfrak{y}_{B^{\prime}}=\frac{j_{B^{\prime}}-{j_{B}}}{e},

forcing
{j_{B^{\prime}}}={j_{B}}
, since
|{j_{B^{\prime}}}-{j_{B}}|<e
, and hence
\mathfrak{y}_{B}=\mathfrak{y}_{B^{\prime}}
.
Thus
\sigma_{B}=\sigma_{B^{\prime}}
and
\mathbf{y}^{B}=\mathbf{y}^{B^{\prime}}
by Corollary
4.19
.
Hence
\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}=\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B^{\prime}}
by Lemma
4.20
,
so that

\mathsf{core}(B)=\mathsf{core}(B^{\prime})
(since
\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}|}(\mathsf{core}(B)))=\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B}}(\boldsymbol{\varnothing})
and similarly for
\mathsf{core}(B^{\prime})
by Lemma
3.1
(1)).
Since
\mathsf{wt}(B)=k=\mathsf{wt}(B^{\prime})
, we see that
B=B^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition 4.22
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
.
There exists a sequence
B_{0},\dotsc,B_{k}
of blocks such that:

(1)

B_{0}=B
, and
B_{k}
is an initial core block with
\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B_{k}}=(\mathbf{v}_{e}(|\mathbf{y}^{B}|)\ell+\mathbf{Sc}(B))^{\rho_{e}^{-j_{B}}}
(recall the notation
\mathbf{v}_{e}(x)
in Subsection
2.1
and
\rho_{e}=(1,2,\dotsc,e)\in\mathfrak{S}_{e}
);

(2)

for each
a\in[0,k-1]
, there exists
j_{a}\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
such that
\mathsf{hub}_{j_{a}}(B_{a})>0
and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
has no addable nodes of
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
j_{a}
for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B_{a}
;

(3)

\mathbf{Sc}(B_{0})=\mathbf{Sc}(B_{1})=\dotsb=\mathbf{Sc}(B_{k})
.

In particular,
B
and
B_{k}
are Scopes equivalent.

Furthermore, under the Scopes equivalence between
B
and
B_{k}
, the Specht module
S^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
of
B
with
\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})=(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*};\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B})
corresponds to the Specht module
S^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
of
B_{k}
with

\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})=((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}\rho_{e}^{-j_{B}}};\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B_{k}}).

Proof.

We prove by induction on
n
.
If
B
is an initial core block, then all statements hold by Lemma
4.20
and Corollary
4.19
.
Assume thus that
B
is not initial.

If
B
does not satisfy Condition (I) of being initial, then there exists
j\in[1,\,e]
such that
y^{B}_{\overline{j+1}}>y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
by Lemma
4.18
(4).

On the other hand, if
B
satisfies Condition (I) but not Condition (II) of being initial,
then
y^{B}_{\overline{j+1}}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
for all
j\in[1,\,e]\setminus\{\overline{j_{B}}\}
by Lemma
4.18
(3),
\sigma_{B}=\rho_{e}^{j_{B}}
by Corollary
4.19
,
and there exists
b\in[1,\,e-1]
such that
z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(b+1)}>z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(b)}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(b+1)})\geq z^{B}_{\sigma_{B}(b)}
by Corollary
4.12
(3).
Let
j=\sigma_{B}(b)
.
Then
j\neq\sigma_{B}(e)=j_{B}
and

\sigma_{B}(b+1)=\rho_{e}^{j_{B}}(\rho_{e}(b))=\rho_{e}(\rho_{e}^{j_{B}}(b))=\rho_{e}(\sigma_{B}(b))=\rho_{e}(j)=\overline{j+1}.

Thus we have
y^{B}_{\overline{j+1}}=y_{j}+\delta_{je}
,
z^{B}_{\overline{j+1}}>z^{B}_{j}
and
\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{\overline{j+1}})\geq z^{B}_{j}
.

Consequently, for both cases, we have
\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)>0
, and
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
has no addable nodes of
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
j
for all
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
by Lemma
4.8
.
Let
B_{1}=\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B
.
Then
B_{1}
is a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n-\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)}
, with
\mathbf{mv}(B)=\mathbf{mv}(B_{1})
by Proposition

3.13
,

|\mathbf{y}^{B_{1}}|=|\mathbf{y}^{B}|
by Lemma
4.7

and
\mathbf{Sc}(B)=\mathbf{Sc}(B_{1})
by Proposition
4.17
.
Since
n-\mathsf{hub}_{j}(B)<n
, by induction, parts (1)–(3) hold for
B_{1}
, and hence for
B
.

We now show the correspondence of
\ell
-partitions lying
B
and
B_{k}
under the Scopes equivalence between them.
For each
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
with
\iota_{\ell,e}((\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})=(\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*};\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B})
, the Specht module
S^{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
lying in
B
corresponds to
S^{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
lying in
B_{1}
by Theorem
4.1
, and

\iota_{\ell,e}((\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})=((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}};\mathbf{r}^{*}_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B})

by Lemma
3.15
(2).
By induction,
S^{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{\mathbf{r}}{\bullet}}\boldsymbol{\lambda}}
corresponds to
S^{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
lying in the initial core block
B_{k}
with

\displaystyle\iota((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})=(((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})^{\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}})^{\sigma_{B_{1}}\tau_{B_{1}}\rho_{e}^{-j_{B_{1}}}};\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B_{k}}).

By Lemma
4.7
,
j_{B_{1}}=j_{\mathsf{s}_{j}\mathrel{\overset{}{\bullet}}B}=j_{B}
.
Furthermore, if
B
does not satisfy Condition (I) of being initial, then
\sigma_{B_{1}}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}
and
\tau_{B_{1}}=\tau_{B}
by Proposition
4.17
(1), and so
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B_{1}}\tau_{B_{1}}=\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}
.
On the other hand,
if
B
satisfies Condition (I) but not Condition (II) of being initial, then setting
a=\sigma_{B}^{-1}(j)
, we have
\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}=\sigma_{B}\mathsf{s}_{a}
by Lemma
4.16
, and

\sigma_{B_{1}}=\sigma_{B}
and
\tau_{B_{1}}=\mathsf{s}_{a}\tau_{B}
by Proposition
4.17
(2),
so that

\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B_{1}}\tau_{B_{1}}=\overline{\mathsf{s}_{j}}\sigma_{B}\mathsf{s}_{a}\tau_{B}=\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}
too.
In both cases, we get

\displaystyle\iota((\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{r})^{w_{0}})=((\boldsymbol{\lambda}^{*})^{\sigma_{B}\tau_{B}\rho_{e}^{-j_{B}}};\mathbf{r}^{*}_{B_{k}})

as desired.
∎

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
B
be a core block of
\mathcal{H}_{n}
. Suppose that there exists
j\in[1,\,e]
such that

y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}\quad\text{ and }\quad z^{B}_{j},z^{B}_{j+1}>\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{j})=\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{j+1}).

Then there exists
\boldsymbol{\lambda}\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
lying in
B
having both an addable node and a removable node, both with
(e;\mathbf{r})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
.

As before,
j+1
is to be read as
1
when
j=e
.

Proof.

Let
(\boldsymbol{\kappa};\mathbf{u})\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}\times\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
be such that
\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{u}}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}))=\beta_{|\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}|}(\mathsf{core}(B))
. Then
\mathbf{u}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}

[
JL21
, Proposition 2.14]
.

Let
i_{1}\in[1,\,\ell]
be such that
\ell-i_{1}=\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{j})
.
Let
i_{2}=\mathrm{max}(\{0\}\cup\{i<i_{1}\mid\mathrm{mv}_{i}(B)=0\})
.
Since
z^{B}_{j},z^{B}_{j+1}>\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{j})=\mathsf{ht}_{I_{B}}(z^{B}_{j+1})
, we have
\ell-i_{2}>z^{B}_{j},z^{B}_{j+1}>\ell-i_{1}
; in particular,
i_{2}+1<i_{1}
.

Now
\mathrm{mv}_{i_{2}+1}(B),\mathrm{mv}_{i_{2}+2}(B),\dotsc,\mathrm{mv}_{i_{1}-1}(B)>0
by definition of
i_{2}
.
Set

\mathbf{v}=(v_{1},\dotsc,v_{\ell}):=\mathbf{mv}(B)-\mathbf{e}_{i_{2}+1}-\mathbf{e}_{i_{2}+2}-\dotsb-\mathbf{e}_{i_{1}-1}.

Then
\mathbf{v}\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{\ell}
.
Let
\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}=(r^{\prime}_{1},\dotsc,r^{\prime}_{\ell})
, and recall that
\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
.
Set
\mathbf{t}=(t_{1},\dotsc,t_{\ell})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
by

t_{a}:=r^{\prime}_{a}-\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a,i_{1}}

for all
a\in[1,\,\ell]
.
Then for
a\in[1,\ell-1]
,

t_{a+1}-t_{a}=r^{\prime}_{a+1}-r^{\prime}_{a}-\delta_{a+1,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a+1,i_{1}}+\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}-\delta_{a,i_{1}},

so that
t_{a+1}\geq t_{a}
when
a\notin\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
.
When
a\in\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
,
since
\mathbf{u}=(u_{1},\dotsc,u_{\ell})\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{e}^{\ell}
and

u_{a}=r^{\prime}_{a}-\mathrm{mv}_{a}(B)+\mathrm{mv}_{a-1}(B)
for all
a\in[1,\ell]

by Corollary
3.5
, we have

\displaystyle t_{a+1}-t_{a}

\displaystyle=r^{\prime}_{a+1}-r^{\prime}_{a}-\delta_{a+1,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a+1,i_{1}}+\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}-\delta_{a,i_{1}}

\displaystyle=u_{a+1}-u_{a}+\mathrm{mv}_{a+1}(B)-2\mathrm{mv}_{a}(B)+\mathrm{mv}_{a-1}(B)-\delta_{a+1,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a+1,i_{1}}+\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}-\delta_{a,i_{1}}

\displaystyle=u_{a+1}-u_{a}+\mathrm{mv}_{a+1}(B)+\mathrm{mv}_{a-1}(B)-\delta_{a+1,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a+1,i_{1}}+\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}-\delta_{a,i_{1}}\geq 0

since
\mathrm{mv}_{i_{1}-1}(B),\mathrm{mv}_{i_{2}+1}(B)\geq 1
.
Consequently,
as

t_{a}=r^{\prime}_{a}-\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a,i_{1}}=u_{a}+\mathrm{mv}_{a}(B)-\mathrm{mv}_{a-1}(B)-\delta_{a,i_{2}+1}+\delta_{a,i_{1}}=u_{a}+v_{a}-v_{a-1},

there exists
\boldsymbol{\mu}=(\mu^{(1)},\dotsc,\mu^{(\ell)})\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
such that
\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{t})=\mathbf{v}
and
\mathsf{core}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{t})=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{u}}(\boldsymbol{\kappa}))
by Corollary
3.8
.

Note that

\displaystyle t_{i_{1}}-t_{i_{2}+1}

\displaystyle=r^{\prime}_{i_{1}}-r^{\prime}_{i_{2}+1}+2

\displaystyle\geq 2;

(4.1)

\displaystyle t_{i_{1}}-t_{a}

\displaystyle=r^{\prime}_{i_{1}}-r^{\prime}_{a}+1

\displaystyle\geq 1;

(4.2)

\displaystyle t_{a}-t_{i_{2}+1}

\displaystyle=r^{\prime}_{a}-r^{\prime}_{i_{2}+1}+1

\displaystyle\geq 1

(4.3)

for all
a\in[i_{2}+2,\,i_{1}-1]
.
In particular, there exist distinct
b_{1},b_{2}\in\beta_{t_{i_{1}}}(\mu^{(i_{1})})\setminus\beta_{t_{i_{2}+1}}(\mu^{(i_{2}+1)})
by Lemma
2.1
(1).

For
k\in\{1,2\}
,
define
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}=(\nu_{k}^{(1)},\dotsc,\nu_{k}^{(\ell)})\in\mathcal{P}^{\ell}
by

\beta_{r^{\prime}_{c}}(\nu_{k}^{(c)})=\begin{cases}\beta_{t_{i_{2}+1}}(\mu^{(i_{2}+1)})\cup\{b_{k}\},&\text{if }c=i_{2}+1;\\
\beta_{t_{i_{1}}}(\mu^{(i_{1})})\setminus\{b_{k}\},&\text{if }c=i_{1};\\
\beta_{t_{c}}(\mu^{(c)}),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then
\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}))
can be obtained from
\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}}(\boldsymbol{\mu}))
by replacing
\upsilon_{i_{1}}(b_{k})
with
\upsilon_{i_{2}+1}(b_{k})
, which equals
\upsilon_{i_{1}}(b_{k})+(i_{1}-(i_{2}+1))e
by Lemma
2.3
(4).
Thus

\displaystyle\mathsf{core}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k};\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{core}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k})))=\mathsf{core}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})))=\mathsf{core}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{t})=\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{u}}(\kappa));

\displaystyle\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k};\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k})))=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{t}}(\boldsymbol{\mu})))+i_{1}-(i_{2}+1)

\displaystyle=|\mathbf{mv}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{t})|+i_{1}-(i_{2}+1)=|\mathbf{mv}(B)|=\mathsf{wt}(B).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathsf{core}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{{w_{0}}^{-1}})=\beta^{-1}(\mathsf{core}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k};\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}))=\beta^{-1}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{u}}(\kappa)))=\mathsf{core}(B);

\displaystyle\mathsf{wt}_{\mathcal{H}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{{w_{0}}^{-1}})=\mathsf{wt}_{e}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k};\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})=\mathsf{wt}(B),

so that
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{{w_{0}}^{-1}}
lies in
B
.

We now consider three separate cases:

Case 1.
\boldsymbol{\mu}
has some addable node and some removable node, both with
(e,\mathbf{t})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
:

If there exists
k\in\{1,2\}
such that
b_{k}\not\equiv_{e}j
and
b_{k}\not\equiv_{e}j-1
, then clearly
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}
has some addable node and some removable node, both with
(e;\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
, inherited from
(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{t})
.

On the other hand, if for all
k\in\{1,2\}
,
b_{k}\equiv_{e}j
or
b_{k}\equiv_{e}j-1
, let
b_{k}=q_{k}e+b^{\prime}_{k}
where
b^{\prime}_{k}\in\mathbb{Z}/e\mathbb{Z}
and
q_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
.
Then

\displaystyle\upsilon_{i_{1}}(b_{k})

\displaystyle=((q_{k}+1)\ell-i_{1})e+b^{\prime}_{k}

\displaystyle\notin\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}));

\displaystyle\upsilon_{i_{2}+1}(b_{k})

\displaystyle=((q_{k}+1)\ell-i_{2}-1)e+b^{\prime}_{k}

\displaystyle\in\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k})).

This implies, by Lemma
4.2
with
i=\ell
, that

\displaystyle(q_{k}+1)\ell-i_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\left\lfloor(x^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1})/\ell\right\rfloor\ell=y^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1}\ell;

\displaystyle(q_{k}+1)\ell-i_{2}-1

\displaystyle<\left\lceil(x^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1})/\ell\right\rceil\ell\leq(y^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1}+1)\ell.

Consequently,

y^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1}\ell-(\ell-i_{1})\leq q_{k}\ell\leq y^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1}\ell+i_{2}

forcing
q_{k}=y^{B}_{b^{\prime}_{k}+1}
since
i_{2},\ell-i_{1}\in[0,\,\ell-1]
.
Since
b_{1}\neq b_{2}
, this forces
b^{\prime}_{1}\neq b^{\prime}_{2}
, and hence
b_{1}\not\equiv_{e}b_{2}
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
b_{1}\equiv_{e}j-1
, and
b_{2}\equiv_{e}j
.
Then
b^{\prime}_{1}=j-1
and
b^{\prime}_{2}=j-\delta_{je}e
, and so

b_{2}=q_{2}e+b^{\prime}_{2}=y^{B}_{j+1}e+j-\delta_{je}e=y^{B}_{j}e+j=q_{1}e+b^{\prime}_{1}+1=b_{1}+1

since
y^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je}
.
Thus moving
b_{2}
from
\beta_{t_{i_{1}}}(\mu^{(i_{1})})
to
\beta_{t_{i_{2}+1}}(\mu^{(i_{2}+1)})
will yield an addable node of
\nu_{2}^{(i_{1})}
with
(e,r^{\prime}_{i_{1}})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
corresponding to
b_{2}
.
Thus
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}
will have an addable node with
(e,\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
, as well as any removable node with
(e,\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
inherited from
\boldsymbol{\mu}
.

In all cases,
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}
has an addable node and a removable node, both with
(e;\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
for some
k\in\{1,2\}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{\lambda}=\boldsymbol{\nu}^{{w_{0}}^{-1}}_{k}
. Then
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
lies in
B
, and has an addable node and a removable node, both with
(e,\mathbf{r})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
.

Case 2.
\boldsymbol{\mu}
has no addable node with
(e,\mathbf{t})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
:

Let
\mathsf{quot}_{e}(\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1})))=(\mathsf{C}_{1},\dotsc,\mathsf{C}_{e})
.
Firstly,

\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})\geq\mathcal{s}(\mathsf{C}_{j})-1=x^{B}_{j}-1=y^{B}_{j}\ell+z^{B}_{j}-1\geq(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell-i_{1}

since
z^{B}_{j}>\ell-i_{1}
so that
z^{B}_{j}-1\geq\ell-i_{1}
.
On the other hand, if
i_{2}>0
, then by Lemma
4.2
with
i=i_{2}
,

\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})<\lceil(x^{B}_{j}+i_{2})/\ell\rceil\ell-i_{2}=\lceil(y^{B}_{j}\ell+z^{B}_{j}+i_{2})/\ell\rceil\ell-i_{2}=(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell-i_{2}

(4.4)

since
0\leq z^{B}_{j}<\ell-i_{2}
.
If
i_{2}=0
, then by Lemma
4.2
with
i=\ell
,

\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})<\lceil x^{B}_{j}/\ell\rceil\ell=\lceil(y^{B}_{j}\ell+z^{B}_{j})/\ell\rceil\ell=(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell=(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell-i_{2}

since
0\leq\ell-i_{1}<z^{B}_{j}<\ell
. Thus, in all cases,

(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell-i_{1}\leq\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})\leq(y^{B}_{j}+1)\ell-i_{2}-1.

Consequently, there exists
a\in[i_{2}+1,\,i_{1}]
such that

\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})=y^{B}_{j}\ell+(\ell-a)
,
so that

\upsilon_{a}(y^{B}_{j}e+j-1)=y^{B}_{j}\ell e+(\ell-a)e+j-1=\mathrm{max}(\mathsf{C}_{j})e+j-1\in\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1})),

and hence
x:=y^{B}_{j}e+j-1\in\beta_{r^{\prime}_{a}}(\nu^{(a)}_{1})
.
This implies that
x\in\beta_{t_{a_{1}}}(\mu^{(a_{1})})
for some
a_{1}\in[i_{2}+1,\,i_{1}]
.
Since
\boldsymbol{\mu}
has no addable node with
(e,\mathbf{t})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
, we have
x+1=y^{B}_{j}e+j\in\beta_{t_{a_{1}}}(\mu^{(a_{1})})
too.

Now,

\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j+1})\leq\mathcal{s}(\mathsf{C}_{j+1})=x^{B}_{j+1}=y^{B}_{j+1}\ell+z^{B}_{j+1}\leq y^{B}_{j+1}\ell+\ell-i_{2}-1.

On the other hand, by Lemma
4.2
with
i=i_{1}
, we have

\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j+1})\geq\lfloor(x^{B}_{j+1}+i_{1})/\ell\rfloor\ell-i_{1}\geq\lfloor(y^{B}_{j+1}\ell+z^{B}_{j+1}+i_{1})/\ell\rfloor\ell-i_{1}=(y^{B}_{j+1}+1)\ell-i_{1}

since
\ell-i_{1}\leq z_{j+1}<\ell
.
Thus, there exists
a^{\prime}\in[i_{2}+1,\,i_{1}]
such that
\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j+1})=y^{B}_{j+1}\ell+(\ell-a^{\prime})
,
so that

\displaystyle\upsilon_{a^{\prime}}(x+1)

\displaystyle=\upsilon_{a^{\prime}}(y^{B}_{j}e+j)=\upsilon_{a^{\prime}}((y^{B}_{j}+\delta_{je})e+j-\delta_{je}e)=\upsilon_{a^{\prime}}(y^{B}_{j+1}e+j-\delta_{je}e)

\displaystyle=y^{B}_{j+1}\ell e+(\ell-a^{\prime})e+j-\delta_{je}e=\mathrm{min}(\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathsf{C}_{j+1})e+j-\delta_{je}e\notin\mathbf{U}(\beta_{\mathbf{r}^{w_{0}}}(\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1})).

Hence
x+1\notin\beta_{r^{\prime}_{a^{\prime}}}(\nu^{(a^{\prime})}_{1})
, and so
x+1\notin\beta_{t_{a_{2}}}(\mu^{(a_{2})})
for some
a_{2}\in[i_{2}+1,i_{1}]
.
Since
(\boldsymbol{\mu};\mathbf{t})
has no addable node with
(e,\mathbf{t})
-residue
\equiv_{e}j
, we have
x\notin\beta_{t_{a_{2}}}(\mu^{(a_{2})})
too.

If
a_{1}<i_{1}
, then
